```
#!/usr/bin/env bash

# Copyright (C) 2018 The noVNC Authors
# Licensed under MPL 2.0 or any later version (see LICENSE.txt)

usage() {
    if [ "$*" ]; then
        echo "$*"
        echo
    fi
    echo "Usage: ${NAME} [--listen PORT] [--vnc VNC_HOST:PORT] [--cert CERT] [--ssl-only]"
    echo
    echo "Starts the WebSockets proxy and a mini-webserver and "
    echo "provides a cut-and-paste URL to go to."
    echo
    echo "    --listen PORT         Port for proxy/webserver to listen on"
    echo "                          Default: 6080"
    echo "    --vnc VNC_HOST:PORT   VNC server host:port proxy target"
    echo "                          Default: localhost:5900"
    echo "    --cert CERT           Path to combined cert/key file, or just"
    echo "                          the cert file if used with --key"
    echo "                          Default: self.pem"
    echo "    --key KEY             Path to key file, when not combined with cert"
    echo "    --web WEB             Path to web files (e.g. vnc.html)"
    echo "                          Default: ./"
    echo "    --ssl-only            Disable non-https connections."
    echo "                                    "
    echo "    --record FILE         Record traffic to FILE.session.js"
    echo "                                    "
    exit 2
}

NAME="$(basename $0)"
REAL_NAME="$(readlink -f $0)"
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "$REAL_NAME")" && pwd)"
PORT="6080"
VNC_DEST="localhost:5900"
CERT=""
KEY=""
WEB=""
proxy_pid=""
SSLONLY=""
RECORD_ARG=""

die() {
    echo "$*"
    exit 1
}

cleanup() {
    trap - TERM QUIT INT EXIT
    trap "true" CHLD   # Ignore cleanup messages
    echo
    if [ -n "${proxy_pid}" ]; then
        echo "Terminating WebSockets proxy (${proxy_pid})"
        kill ${proxy_pid}
    fi
}

# Process Arguments

# Arguments that only apply to chrooter itself
while [ "$*" ]; do
    param=$1; shift; OPTARG=$1
    case $param in
    --listen)  PORT="${OPTARG}"; shift            ;;
    --vnc)     VNC_DEST="${OPTARG}"; shift        ;;
    --cert)    CERT="${OPTARG}"; shift            ;;
    --key)     KEY="${OPTARG}"; shift             ;;
    --web)     WEB="${OPTARG}"; shift            ;;
    --ssl-only) SSLONLY="--ssl-only"             ;;
    --record) RECORD_ARG="--record ${OPTARG}"; shift ;;
    -h|--help) usage                              ;;
    -*) usage "Unknown chrooter option: ${param}" ;;
    *) break                                      ;;
    esac
done

# Sanity checks
if bash -c "exec 7<>/dev/tcp/localhost/${PORT}" &> /dev/null; then
    exec 7<&-
    exec 7>&-
    die "Port ${PORT} in use. Try --listen PORT"
else
    exec 7<&-
    exec 7>&-
fi

trap "cleanup" TERM QUIT INT EXIT

# Find vnc.html
if [ -n "${WEB}" ]; then
    if [ ! -e "${WEB}/vnc.html" ]; then
        die "Could not find ${WEB}/vnc.html"
    fi
elif [ -e "$(pwd)/vnc.html" ]; then
    WEB=$(pwd)
elif [ -e "${HERE}/../vnc.html" ]; then
    WEB=${HERE}/../
elif [ -e "${HERE}/vnc.html" ]; then
    WEB=${HERE}
elif [ -e "${HERE}/../share/novnc/vnc.html" ]; then
    WEB=${HERE}/../share/novnc/
else
    die "Could not find vnc.html"
fi

# Find self.pem
if [ -n "${CERT}" ]; then
    if [ ! -e "${CERT}" ]; then
        die "Could not find ${CERT}"
    fi
elif [ -e "$(pwd)/self.pem" ]; then
    CERT="$(pwd)/self.pem"
elif [ -e "${HERE}/../self.pem" ]; then
    CERT="${HERE}/../self.pem"
elif [ -e "${HERE}/self.pem" ]; then
    CERT="${HERE}/self.pem"
else
    echo "Warning: could not find self.pem"
fi

# Check key file
if [ -n "${KEY}" ]; then
    if [ ! -e "${KEY}" ]; then
        die "Could not find ${KEY}"
    fi
fi

# try to find websockify (prefer local, try global, then download local)
if [[ -d ${HERE}/websockify ]]; then
    WEBSOCKIFY=${HERE}/websockify/run

    if [[ ! -x $WEBSOCKIFY ]]; then
        echo "The path ${HERE}/websockify exists, but $WEBSOCKIFY either does not exist or is not executable."
        echo "If you intended to use an installed websockify package, please remove ${HERE}/websockify."
        exit 1
    fi

    echo "Using local websockify at $WEBSOCKIFY"
else
    WEBSOCKIFY_FROMSYSTEM=$(which websockify 2>/dev/null)
    WEBSOCKIFY_FROMSNAP=${HERE}/../usr/bin/python2-websockify
    [ -f $WEBSOCKIFY_FROMSYSTEM ] && WEBSOCKIFY=$WEBSOCKIFY_FROMSYSTEM
    [ -f $WEBSOCKIFY_FROMSNAP ] && WEBSOCKIFY=$WEBSOCKIFY_FROMSNAP

    if [ ! -f "$WEBSOCKIFY" ]; then
        echo "No installed websockify, attempting to clone websockify..."
        WEBSOCKIFY=${HERE}/websockify/run
        git clone https://github.com/novnc/websockify ${HERE}/websockify

        if [[ ! -e $WEBSOCKIFY ]]; then
            echo "Unable to locate ${HERE}/websockify/run after downloading"
            exit 1
        fi

        echo "Using local websockify at $WEBSOCKIFY"
    else
        echo "Using installed websockify at $WEBSOCKIFY"
    fi
fi

echo "Starting webserver and WebSockets proxy on port ${PORT}"
#${HERE}/websockify --web ${WEB} ${CERT:+--cert ${CERT}} ${PORT} ${VNC_DEST} &
${WEBSOCKIFY} ${SSLONLY} --web ${WEB} ${CERT:+--cert ${CERT}} ${KEY:+--key ${KEY}} ${PORT} ${VNC_DEST} ${RECORD_ARG} &
proxy_pid="$!"
sleep 1
if ! ps -p ${proxy_pid} >/dev/null; then
    proxy_pid=
    echo "Failed to start WebSockets proxy"
    exit 1
fi

echo -e "\n\nNavigate to this URL:\n"
if [ "x$SSLONLY" == "x" ]; then
    echo -e "    http://$(hostname):${PORT}/vnc.html?host=$(hostname)&port=${PORT}\n"
else
    echo -e "    https://$(hostname):${PORT}/vnc.html?host=$(hostname)&port=${PORT}\n"
fi

echo -e "Press Ctrl-C to exit\n\n"

wait ${proxy_pid}
```

> AGENT

Connection timed out - x11vnc is waiting for the client to send data first (new WebSocket-detection behavior in 0.9.17: reads first bytes before sending RFB banner). Deadlock: websockify also waits for x11vnc to speak first.

Let me verify this is the issue and check x11vnc flags to disable WebSocket detection:

> TOOL

> AGENT

The root cause: x11vnc 0.9.17 (2025) added WebSocket detection on the VNC port — it waits for the client to send data first to detect if it's HTTP/WebSocket vs raw RFB. This creates a deadlock with websockify (which waits for the RFB banner first). The fix is `-nohttp` to disable HTTP/WebSocket detection so x11vnc immediately sends the RFB banner.

Let me test this live:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01JoZDvhVjzUQa8N1H75RDCo

  "command": "ssh sandcastle@sandman 'docker exec thies-slow-tiger bash -c \"kill \\$(pgrep x11vnc); sleep 1; DISPLAY=:99 x11vnc -shared -forever -nopw -rfbport 5900 -nohttp &>/var/log/x11vnc.log &\"; sleep 2; ssh sandcastle@sandman \"docker exec thies-slow-tiger pgrep x11vnc && echo running\"'",
  "description": "Restart x11vnc with -nohttp flag in sandbox container"
}
```

id: toolu_01SMufPXa7ccJ8j8T4aUVhpw

> AGENT

x11vnc 0.9.17 blocks indefinitely waiting for client data (WebSocket detection). It never sends the RFB banner first — completely deadlocking with websockify.

The fix is to replace x11vnc with **TigerVNC's Xvnc**, which sends the RFB banner immediately. Let me test it live first, then update the Dockerfile:

> TOOL

     6→
     7→# System tools

     9→    openssh-server sudo curl git tmux vim neovim \
    10→    build-essential \
    11→    jq ripgrep fd-find htop wget unzip ca-certificates net-tools iputils-ping \
    12→    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
    13→
    14→# GUI tools: Xvfb (virtual X server), x11vnc, and Google Chrome
    15→RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y \
    16→    xvfb x11vnc xfonts-base xfonts-100dpi xfonts-75dpi \
    17→    fonts-liberation libappindicator3-1 libasound2t64 libatk-bridge2.0-0t64 \
    18→    libatk1.0-0t64 libcups2t64 libdbus-1-3 libgdk-pixbuf-2.0-0 libnspr4 libnss3 \
    19→    libx11-xcb1 libxcomposite1 libxdamage1 libxrandr2 xdg-utils \
    20→    && wget -q https://dl.google.com/linux/direct/google-chrome-stable_current_amd64.deb \
    21→    && apt-get install -y ./google-chrome-stable_current_amd64.deb \
    22→    && rm google-chrome-stable_current_amd64.deb \
    23→    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
    24→
    25→# Prefer IPv4 to avoid slow/broken IPv6 connections
    26→RUN sed -i 's/#precedence ::ffff:0:0\/96  100/precedence ::ffff:0:0\/96  100/' /etc/gai.conf
    27→
    28→# Docker CLI + daemon (Sysbox makes this safe)
    29→RUN curl -fsSL https://get.docker.com | sh
    30→
    31→# Pin runc to 1.1.x — runc 1.2+ added a procfs safety check that fails
    32→# inside sysbox containers (sysbox-fs mounts /proc/sys as separate FUSE)
    33→RUN RUNC_VERSION="v1.1.15" \
    34→    && ARCH=$(dpkg --print-architecture) \
    35→    && curl -fsSL "https://github.com/opencontainers/runc/releases/download/${RUNC_VERSION}/runc.${ARCH}" \
    36→       -o /usr/bin/runc \
    37→    && chmod +x /usr/bin/runc
    38→
    39→# Docker Compose plugin
    40→RUN mkdir -p /usr/local/lib/docker/cli-plugins \
    41→    && curl -fsSL "https://github.com/docker/compose/releases/latest/download/docker-compose-linux-$(uname -m)" \
    42→       -o /usr/local/lib/docker/cli-plugins/docker-compose \
    43→    && chmod +x /usr/local/lib/docker/cli-plugins/docker-compose
    44→
    45→# Stage mise + Claude Code in /opt/sandcastle/bin (seeded to ~/.local/bin on first boot)
    46→RUN mkdir -p /opt/sandcastle/bin \
    47→    && curl https://mise.run | REDACTED sh \
    48→    && curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.sh | bash \
    49→    && cp -L /root/.local/bin/claude /opt/sandcastle/bin/claude \
    50→    && rm -rf /root/.local/share/claude /root/.local/bin/claude
    51→
    52→# Entire CLI — git observability for AI agent sessions (https://entire.io)
    53→RUN ARCH="$(dpkg --print-architecture)" \
    54→    && curl -fsSL "https://github.com/entireio/cli/releases/latest/download/entire_linux_${ARCH}.tar.gz" \
    55→       | tar -xz -C /usr/local/bin entire \
    56→    && chmod +x /usr/local/bin/entire
    57→
    58→# Shell config: PATH, mise activation, aliases
    59→RUN echo 'export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH"' >> /etc/bash.bashrc \
    60→    && echo 'eval "$(mise activate bash)"' >> /etc/bash.bashrc \
    61→    && echo "alias yolo='claude --dangerously-skip-permissions'" >> /etc/bash.bashrc
    62→
    63→# Bake version into the image
    64→RUN echo "${SANDCASTLE_VERSION}" > /etc/sandcastle-version
    65→
    66→# SSH configuration (key-only auth) — use drop-in config for Ubuntu 25.10 compatibility
    67→RUN mkdir -p /var/run/sshd /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d \
    68→    && echo "PermitRootLogin no" > /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/10-sandcastle.conf \
    69→    && echo "PubkeyAuthentication yes" >> /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/10-sandcastle.conf \
    70→    && echo "PasswordAuthentication no" >> /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/10-sandcastle.conf
    71→
    72→COPY entrypoint.sh /entrypoint.sh
    73→RUN chmod +x /entrypoint.sh
    74→
    75→COPY tmux.conf /etc/tmux.conf
    76→COPY gitconfig /etc/gitconfig
    77→
    78→LABEL org.opencontainers.image.source="https://github.com/thieso2/sandcastle"
    79→LABEL org.opencontainers.image.description="Sandcastle sandbox image — Ubuntu 25.10 with Docker-in-Docker, SSH, and dev tools"
    80→LABEL org.opencontainers.image.licenses="MIT"

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh",
  "old_string": "# Start virtual X server and VNC server for browser access\nif command -v Xvfb &>/dev/null && command -v x11vnc &>/dev/null; then\n    # Start Xvfb on display :99 with 1920x1080 resolution and 24-bit color depth\n    Xvfb :99 -screen 0 1920x1080x24 &>/var/log/xvfb.log &\n    # Wait a moment for Xvfb to initialize\n    sleep 1\n    # Start x11vnc server: -shared allows multiple connections, -forever keeps running,\n    # -nopw allows connections without password (secured via Docker network isolation)\n    # WebSocket translation is handled by the gotget/novnc sidecar (websockify).\n    DISPLAY=:99 x11vnc -shared -forever -nopw -rfbport 5900 &>/var/log/x11vnc.log &\nfi",
  "new_string": "# Start virtual X + VNC server for browser access.\n# Xvnc (TigerVNC) combines Xvfb and a VNC server in a single process and sends\n# the RFB banner immediately on connect — required for websockify compatibility.\n# x11vnc 0.9.17+ waits for client data before sending the banner, deadlocking\n# with websockify which also waits for the server to speak first.\nif command -v Xvnc &>/dev/null; then\n    Xvnc :99 -rfbport 5900 -SecurityTypes None -AlwaysShared \\\n        -geometry 1920x1080 -depth 24 \\\n        &>/var/log/xvnc.log &\nfi"
}

This deadlocks with websockify, which\nwaits for the server to speak first per the RFB protocol.\n\nXvnc (TigerVNC) combines the virtual X server and VNC server in one\nprocess and sends the RFB banner immediately on connect, working\ncorrectly with websockify.\n\n- Dockerfile: tigervnc-standalone-server replaces xvfb + x11vnc\n- entrypoint.sh: Xvnc :99 replaces Xvfb :99 + x11vnc\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>\" -- images/sandbox/Dockerfile images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh",

}

> AGENT

The RFB banner arrives in 0ms. Try opening the VNC session in your browser now — it should connect immediately.

**Summary of what was wrong and what was fixed:**

| Layer | Old | New |
|---|---|---|
| VNC server | `x11vnc` 0.9.17 — waits indefinitely for client to speak first (WebSocket detection deadlock) | `Xvnc` (TigerVNC 1.15) — sends RFB banner immediately |
| Dockerfile | `xvfb` + `x11vnc` | `tigervnc-standalone-server` |
| entrypoint.sh | `Xvfb :99` + `x11vnc -display :99` | `Xvnc :99 -rfbport 5900` (single process) |
| URL/path | `/vnc/{id}/novnc/vnc.html?path=.../novnc/websockify` | `/vnc/{id}/vnc.html?path=.../websockify` |

The new sandbox image will include TigerVNC when the GitHub Actions release workflow runs. For existing sandboxes (like `thies-slow-tiger`), the live fix is already in place — Xvnc is running and accepting connections instantly.

Two places — both `HostConfig` blocks need `ShmSize`. 2GB = `2 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024` bytes:

> TOOL

id: toolu_01XdeLN4aj3dDgBE2aD6p7qR
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "      \"HostConfig\" => {\n        \"Runtime\" => container_runtime,\n        \"PortBindings\" => {\n          \"22/tcp\" => [ { \"HostPort\" => sandbox.ssh_port.to_s } ]\n        },\n        \"Binds\" => volume_binds(user, sandbox),\n        \"RestartPolicy\" => { \"Name\" => \"unless-stopped\" }\n      }\n    )\n\n    container.start\n    container.refresh!\n    raise Error, \"Container failed to start (state: #{container.json.dig(\"State\", \"Status\")})\" unless container.json.dig(\"State\", \"Running\")",
  "new_string": "      \"HostConfig\" => {\n        \"Runtime\" => container_runtime,\n        \"PortBindings\" => {\n          \"22/tcp\" => [ { \"HostPort\" => sandbox.ssh_port.to_s } ]\n        },\n        \"Binds\" => volume_binds(user, sandbox),\n        \"RestartPolicy\" => { \"Name\" => \"unless-stopped\" },\n        \"ShmSize\" => 2 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 # 2GB for Chrome/Chromium shared memory\n      }\n    )\n\n    container.start\n    container.refresh!\n    raise Error, \"Container failed to start (state: #{container.json.dig(\"State\", \"Status\")})\" unless container.json.dig(\"State\", \"Running\")"
}

     1→#!/bin/bash
     2→set -e
     3→
     4→USERNAME="${SANDCASTLE_USER:-sandbox}"
     5→SSH_KEY="${SANDCASTLE_SSH_KEY:-}"
     6→
     7→# Create user if not exists
     8→if ! id "$USERNAME" &>/dev/null; then
     9→    useradd -m -s /bin/bash -G sudo,docker "$USERNAME"
    10→    echo "$USERNAME ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD:ALL" >> /etc/sudoers.d/sandcastle
    11→    chmod 0440 /etc/sudoers.d/sandcastle
    12→fi
    13→
    14→# Set up SSH authorized keys (append if not already present, preserving
    15→# any WeTTY keys that may have been injected for other sandboxes sharing
    16→# this user's home directory via bind mount).
    17→if [ -n "$SSH_KEY" ]; then
    18→    SSH_DIR="/home/$USERNAME/.ssh"
    19→    mkdir -p "$SSH_DIR"
    20→    if [ -f "$SSH_DIR/authorized_keys" ]; then
    21→        grep -qF "$SSH_KEY" "$SSH_DIR/authorized_keys" || echo "$SSH_KEY" >> "$SSH_DIR/authorized_keys"
    22→    else
    23→        echo "$SSH_KEY" > "$SSH_DIR/authorized_keys"
    24→    fi
    25→    chmod 700 "$SSH_DIR"
    26→    chmod 600 "$SSH_DIR/authorized_keys"
    27→    chown -R "$USERNAME:$USERNAME" "$SSH_DIR"
    28→fi
    29→
    30→# Ensure home directory ownership and permissions.
    31→# The host may create bind-mounted home dirs with 777 so Sysbox-mapped root
    32→# can write. Tighten to 755 here so sshd StrictModes is satisfied.
    33→chown "$USERNAME:$USERNAME" "/home/$USERNAME"
    34→chmod 755 "/home/$USERNAME"
    35→
    36→# Ensure workspace is accessible
    37→chown "$USERNAME:$USERNAME" /workspace 2>/dev/null || true
    38→
    39→# Seed mise + Claude Code into user's ~/.local/bin on first boot
    40→USER_LOCAL_BIN="/home/$USERNAME/.local/bin"
    41→mkdir -p "$USER_LOCAL_BIN"
    42→for tool in mise claude; do
    43→    if [ ! -f "$USER_LOCAL_BIN/$tool" ] && [ -f "/opt/sandcastle/bin/$tool" ]; then
    44→        cp "/opt/sandcastle/bin/$tool" "$USER_LOCAL_BIN/$tool"
    45→    fi
    46→done
    47→chown -R "$USERNAME:$USERNAME" "/home/$USERNAME/.local"
    48→
    49→# Configure git identity system-wide if provided
    50→if [ -n "$USER_FULLNAME" ] || [ -n "$USER_EMAIL" ]; then
    51→    {
    52→        echo "[user]"
    53→        [ -n "$USER_FULLNAME" ] && echo "    name = $USER_FULLNAME"
    54→        [ -n "$USER_EMAIL" ] && echo "    email = $USER_EMAIL"
    55→    } >> /etc/gitconfig
    56→fi
    57→
    58→# Generate SSH host keys if missing
    59→ssh-keygen -A
    60→
    61→# Start Docker daemon in background (requires Sysbox runtime for isolated /var/lib/docker)
    62→# Don't wait for it to be ready - users can check with `docker info` after SSH login
    63→if command -v dockerd &>/dev/null && [ -e /dev/fuse ]; then
    64→    # Match inner Docker bridge MTU to container's eth0 to avoid packet fragmentation
    65→    ETH0_MTU=$(ip link show eth0 2>/dev/null | grep -oP 'mtu \K[0-9]+' || echo 1500)

    69→fi
    70→
    71→# Start virtual X + VNC server for browser access.
    72→# Xvnc (TigerVNC) combines Xvfb and a VNC server in a single process and sends
    73→# the RFB banner immediately on connect — required for websockify compatibility.
    74→# x11vnc 0.9.17+ waits for client data before sending the banner, deadlocking
    75→# with websockify which also waits for the server to speak first.
    76→if command -v Xvnc &>/dev/null; then
    77→    Xvnc :99 -rfbport 5900 -SecurityTypes None -AlwaysShared \
    78→        -geometry 1920x1080 -depth 24 \
    79→        &>/var/log/xvnc.log &
    80→fi

    82→# Start SSH daemon in foreground

## 1. WeTTY Sidecar (`sc-wetty-{user}-{sandbox}`)

**File:** `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/terminal_manager.rb`

### Constants
- `WETTY_IMAGE` = `wettyoss/wetty:latest` (overridable via `SANDCASTLE_WETTY_IMAGE`)
- `NETWORK_NAME` = `"sandcastle-web"` (the same bridge network Rails and Traefik are on)
- `DYNAMIC_DIR` = `/data/traefik/dynamic`

### `open(sandbox:)` lifecycle

1. Guard: sandbox must be `"running"` and have a `container_id`.
2. Idempotency: if a container named `sc-wetty-{full_name}` is already running, return the URL immediately without doing anything.
3. `pull_image` — calls `Docker::Image.get(WETTY_IMAGE)`, pulls if not found.
4. `ensure_network` — calls `Docker::Network.get("sandcastle-web")`, creates if missing.
5. `connect_sandbox_to_network(sandbox)` — reads the sandbox container's existing networks, calls `network.connect(sandbox.container_id)` only if it is not already on `sandcastle-web`. This makes the sandbox container reachable by DNS name (`sandbox.full_name`) from within the `sandcastle-web` network.
6. `write_traefik_config(sandbox)` — writes a YAML file to `/data/traefik/dynamic/terminal-{id}.yml` immediately (before the container exists) so Traefik's file watcher has a head start.
7. `generate_keypair(sandbox)` — runs `ssh-keygen -t ed25519` on the Rails host (inside the `sandcastle-web` container), producing `key` and `key.pub` under `/data/wetty/{full_name}/`. Perms: key dir 700, private key 600.
8. `inject_pubkey(sandbox, key_dir)` — does three `docker exec` calls on the sandbox container:
   - `mkdir -p /home/{username}/.ssh`
   - `sh -c "echo {base64-pubkey} | base64 -d >> /home/{username}/.ssh/authorized_keys"` (append, not overwrite)
   - `chown -R {username}:{username} /home/{username}/.ssh` + `chmod 600 authorized_keys`
9. `create_wetty_container(...)`:
   - Removes any stale container with the same name.
   - Calls `Docker::Container.create(...)` with:
     - `"name"` = `"sc-wetty-{full_name}"`
     - `"Image"` = `wettyoss/wetty:latest`
     - `"Env"` = `["COMMAND=ssh -p 22 -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null -o LogLevel=ERROR -i /etc/wetty/key {username}@{full_name} -t tmux new-session -A -s main", "BASE=/terminal/{id}/wetty"]`
     - `"HostConfig"` = `NetworkMode: sandcastle-web`, `RestartPolicy: no`, Memory 128 MB, 0.5 CPU
     - `"Labels"` = `{ sandcastle.sandbox_id: id, sandcastle.role: wetty }`
   - Calls `container.start`
   - Calls `copy_key_to_container(container, key_dir)`:
     - `docker exec mkdir -p /etc/wetty`
     - `docker exec sh -c "echo {base64-privkey} | base64 -d > /etc/wetty/key"` (no bind mount because Rails' `/data` is a named volume whose host path doesn't match)
     - `docker exec chmod 600 /etc/wetty/key`
10. `sleep 2` — lets Traefik detect the new dynamic config.
11. Returns `/terminal/{id}/wetty`.

### Traefik config written (`terminal-{id}.yml`)

```yaml
http:
  routers:
    terminal-{id}:
      rule: "Host(`{host}`) && PathPrefix(`/terminal/{id}/wetty`)"
      service: terminal-{id}
      entryPoints: [websecure]
      tls: {certResolver: letsencrypt}
      middlewares: [terminal-auth-{id}]
      priority: 100
  middlewares:
    terminal-auth-{id}:
      forwardAuth:
        address: "http://sandcastle-web:80/terminal/auth"
        trustForwardHeader: true
  services:
    terminal-{id}:
      loadBalancer:
        servers: [{url: "http://sc-wetty-{full_name}:3000"}]
```

No `stripPrefix` middleware — WeTTY serves at its own base path set by the `BASE` env var.

### `close(sandbox:)` lifecycle

1. `Docker::Container.get(name)`, `stop(t:3)`, `delete(force:true)`.
2. `delete_traefik_config` — deletes `/data/traefik/dynamic/terminal-{id}.yml`.
3. `FileUtils.rm_rf(key_dir)` — removes `/data/wetty/{full_name}/`.
4. `remove_pubkey(sandbox)` — `docker exec grep -v 'wetty-{full_name}$' authorized_keys > /tmp/ak_clean && mv ...` — removes the WeTTY key by its comment marker, using `$` anchor to avoid removing keys of sandboxes whose names are prefixes of other sandboxes.

### State maintained

- SSH keypair on Rails host at `/data/wetty/{full_name}/key[.pub]` (temporary, deleted on close)
- Public key appended to sandbox's `authorized_keys` (removed on close)
- Traefik YAML file at `/data/traefik/dynamic/terminal-{id}.yml`
- Container labeled with `sandcastle.sandbox_id` for orphan cleanup

---

## 2. noVNC Sidecar (`sc-vnc-{user}-{sandbox}`)

**File:** `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/vnc_manager.rb`

### Constants
- `NOVNC_IMAGE` = `gotget/novnc:latest` (overridable via `SANDCASTLE_NOVNC_IMAGE`)
- `NETWORK_NAME` = `"sandcastle-web"`
- `DYNAMIC_DIR` = `/data/traefik/dynamic`

### `open(sandbox:)` lifecycle

1. Guard: sandbox must be `"running"` and have a `container_id`.
2. Idempotency: if container `sc-vnc-{full_name}` already running, return URL immediately.
3. `pull_image` — same pattern as WeTTY.
4. `ensure_network` — same as WeTTY.
5. `connect_sandbox_to_network(sandbox)` — identical to WeTTY's version (attaches sandbox container to `sandcastle-web`).
6. `write_traefik_config(sandbox)` — writes `/data/traefik/dynamic/vnc-{id}.yml`.
7. `create_novnc_container(sandbox:, user:)`:
   - Removes any stale container.
   - `Docker::Container.create(...)` with:
     - `"name"` = `"sc-vnc-{full_name}"`
     - `"Image"` = `gotget/novnc:latest`
     - `"Cmd"` = `["--vnc", "{full_name}:5900", "--listen", "6080"]` — tells the image's `launch.sh` to proxy VNC from the sandbox at port 5900, serve on port 6080
     - `"HostConfig"` = `NetworkMode: sandcastle-web`, `RestartPolicy: no`, Memory 128 MB, 0.25 CPU
     - `"Labels"` = `{ sandcastle.sandbox_id: id, sandcastle.role: novnc }`
   - `container.start`
   - No key injection needed — VNC server inside sandbox runs with `SecurityTypes None`.
8. `sleep 2` — Traefik detection window.
9. Returns `/vnc/{id}/vnc.html?path=vnc/{id}/websockify`.

### Traefik config written (`vnc-{id}.yml`)

```yaml
http:
  routers:
    vnc-{id}:
      rule: "Host(`{host}`) && PathPrefix(`/vnc/{id}`)"
      service: vnc-{id}
      entryPoints: [websecure]
      tls: {certResolver: letsencrypt}
      middlewares: [vnc-auth-{id}, vnc-stripprefix-{id}]
      priority: 100
  middlewares:
    vnc-auth-{id}:
      forwardAuth:
        address: "http://sandcastle-web:80/vnc/auth"
        trustForwardHeader: true
    vnc-stripprefix-{id}:
      stripPrefix:
        prefixes: ["/vnc/{id}"]
  services:
    vnc-{id}:
      loadBalancer:
        servers: [{url: "http://sc-vnc-{full_name}:6080"}]
```

Key difference from WeTTY: noVNC uses a `stripPrefix` middleware so that after stripping `/vnc/{id}`, the noVNC container receives paths like `/vnc.html` and `/websockify` that its internal `launch.sh` webserver and websockify expect.

### `close(sandbox:)` lifecycle

1. Stop and delete the noVNC container.
2. Delete `/data/traefik/dynamic/vnc-{id}.yml`.
3. No key cleanup (VNC has no auth keys).

### State maintained

- Traefik YAML file at `/data/traefik/dynamic/vnc-{id}.yml`
- Container labeled with `sandcastle.sandbox_id`
- No persistent key material

### Note on VncController `auth` regex

The `VncController#auth` regex is `%r{/vnc/(\d+)/novnc}` but VncManager's `vnc_url` returns `/vnc/{id}/vnc.html?path=vnc/{id}/websockify` — not a `/novnc/` path segment. Traefik's forwardAuth sends requests to `/vnc/auth` with `X-Forwarded-Uri` set to the original request path. The path the browser actually hits is `/vnc/{id}/vnc.html`, which does not contain `/novnc`, meaning the regex in `VncController#auth` would fail to match and return `401` for all VNC requests. This appears to be a bug: the regex expects a `/novnc` segment that the current URL scheme does not produce.

---

## 3. Sandbox networking: `connect_sandbox_to_network`

This method exists independently in both `TerminalManager` and `VncManager` (not delegated to `SandboxManager`). Both implementations are identical:

```ruby
def connect_sandbox_to_network(sandbox)
  return unless sandbox.container_id.present?
  network = Docker::Network.get(NETWORK_NAME)          # "sandcastle-web"
  container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
  networks = container.json.dig("NetworkSettings", "Networks") || {}
  return if networks.key?(NETWORK_NAME)                # idempotent
  network.connect(sandbox.container_id)
rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
  raise Error, "Sandbox container not found."
end
```

The sandbox container is normally on its own default Docker network (no network specified at creation time in `SandboxManager`). When a terminal or VNC session is opened, the sandbox is dynamically attached to `sandcastle-web`. This means the WeTTY or noVNC sidecar (also on `sandcastle-web`) can reach the sandbox by its `full_name` hostname (e.g., `alice-mybox`), which Docker's internal DNS resolves to the sandbox container's IP on that network.

The `SandboxManager` itself does not have a `connect_sandbox_to_network` method — sandbox containers are created with no explicit network, landing on Docker's default bridge. The network attachment to `sandcastle-web` only happens on-demand when a sidecar opens.

---

## 4. Rails container (`sandcastle-web`)

**Files:** `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/Dockerfile`, `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/docker-compose.yml`

### Dockerfile (production stage)

- Base: `ruby:4.0.0-slim`
- Runtime packages: `curl libjemalloc2 libvips libpq5 postgresql-client openssh-client`
- Docker CLI installed from Docker's apt repo (not the full daemon, CLI only)
- Also installs: `sudo btrfs-progs`
- User: `sandcastle` uid/gid `220568`, added to the `docker` group
- Sudoers entry: `sandcastle ALL=(root) NOPASSWD: /usr/bin/btrfs subvolume *` plus `/usr/bin/chown`
- No Docker socket mounted in the image itself — that comes from `docker-compose.yml`
- Entrypoint: `/rails/bin/docker-entrypoint` (runs `db:prepare` before boot)
- CMD: `./bin/thrust ./bin/rails server` (Thruster HTTP proxy + Puma, port 80)
- Exposes port 80

### docker-compose.yml

- Container name: `sandcastle-web`
- `group_add: ["${DOCKER_GID:-988}"]` — adds the host Docker group so the `sandcastle` user inside can access the mounted socket
- `cap_add: [SYS_ADMIN]` — needed for BTRFS subvolume operations
- Volumes:
  - `/var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock` — allows calling the Docker API to manage sandbox, WeTTY, and noVNC containers
  - `/data:/data` — gives access to the same `/data` tree that Traefik dynamic configs and SSH keys land in
- Network: `sandcastle-web` (external bridge, defined once, shared with Traefik and all sidecars)
- Key env vars: `SANDCASTLE_HOST`, `SANDCASTLE_DATA_DIR=/data`, `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE`

The Rails app talks to the Docker daemon exclusively via the mounted socket at `/var/run/docker.sock` using the `docker-api` gem. All containers it creates (sandboxes, WeTTY, noVNC) therefore live on the same Docker host.

---

## 5. Sandbox image (`images/sandbox/`)

**Files:** `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/images/sandbox/Dockerfile`, `entrypoint.sh`

### Dockerfile

- Base: `ubuntu:25.10`
- SSH server: openssh-server, key-only auth enforced via `/etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/10-sandcastle.conf` (`PubkeyAuthentication yes`, `PasswordAuthentication no`, `PermitRootLogin no`)
- GUI stack: `tigervnc-standalone-server` (`Xvnc`), xfonts, Google Chrome stable, libx11 dependencies
- Docker full install (CLI + daemon) via `curl https://get.docker.com | sh`
- runc pinned to v1.1.15 (runc 1.2+ breaks inside Sysbox due to procfs safety checks)
- Docker Compose plugin installed at `/usr/local/lib/docker/cli-plugins/`
- `mise` and `claude` (Claude Code CLI) pre-staged at `/opt/sandcastle/bin/`
- `entire` CLI at `/usr/local/bin/entire`
- IPv4 preferred in `/etc/gai.conf`
- Exposes ports `22` (SSH) and `5900` (VNC/RFB)
- Entrypoint: `/entrypoint.sh`

### entrypoint.sh processes

1. Creates the Unix user `$SANDCASTLE_USER` (default `sandbox`) with groups `sudo,docker` and passwordless sudo if not already present.
2. Appends `$SANDCASTLE_SSH_KEY` to `~/.ssh/authorized_keys` (grep for duplicate before appending, preserves WeTTY injected keys from other sandboxes sharing the same bind-mounted home).
3. Fixes home dir perms: `chown {user}:{user}`, `chmod 755` (satisfies sshd StrictModes).
4. Seeds `mise` and `claude` from `/opt/sandcastle/bin/` to `~/.local/bin/` on first boot.
5. Appends `USER_FULLNAME` / `USER_EMAIL` to `/etc/gitconfig` if provided.
6. Runs `ssh-keygen -A` to generate SSH host keys if missing.
7. Remounts `/dev/shm` to 2 GB (for Chrome; works because Sysbox gives each container an isolated kernel).
8. Starts `dockerd --storage-driver=overlay2 --mtu={eth0_mtu}` in background (only if `dockerd` exists and `/dev/fuse` is available — i.e., running under Sysbox).
9. Starts `Xvnc :99 -rfbport 5900 -SecurityTypes None -AlwaysShared -geometry 1920x1080 -depth 24` in background (if `Xvnc` is available). Display `:99`, VNC port 5900, no auth.
10. Execs `/usr/sbin/sshd -D -e` as the foreground process.

Container env vars passed by `SandboxManager`:
- `SANDCASTLE_USER={username}` — controls which Unix user is created
- `SANDCASTLE_SSH_KEY={pubkey}` — user's permanent SSH public key
- `USER_EMAIL={email}` — optional, goes into gitconfig
- `USER_FULLNAME={name}` — optional, goes into gitconfig

---

## 6. Traefik forwardAuth endpoints

### `TerminalController#auth`
**File:** `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/controllers/terminal_controller.rb`

- Marked `allow_unauthenticated_access only: :auth` (skips normal auth requirement).
- `skip_before_action :require_password_change, only: :auth`.
- Traefik calls `GET http://sandcastle-web:80/terminal/auth` with header `X-Forwarded-Uri: /terminal/{id}/wetty`.
- Extracts `id` by matching `%r{/terminal/(\d+)/wetty}` against `X-Forwarded-Uri`.
- If no match: `head :unauthorized`.
- Looks up session: `Session.find_by(id: cookies.signed[:session_id])` — reads the browser's `session_id` cookie which Traefik forwards.
- If no session: saves `return_to_after_authenticating` in the Rails session, redirects to `/sessions/new`. Traefik passes this redirect through to the browser.
- If session valid: checks `sandbox.user_id == user.id || user.admin?`. If not: `head :unauthorized`. If yes: `head :ok`.
- Traefik's `trustForwardHeader: true` ensures the forwarded headers (proto, host, URI) are trusted.

### `VncController#auth`
**File:** `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/controllers/vnc_controller.rb`

- Identical structure.
- Traefik calls `GET http://sandcastle-web:80/vnc/auth` with `X-Forwarded-Uri`.
- Regex used: `%r{/vnc/(\d+)/novnc}`.
- Same session cookie lookup, sandbox ownership check, redirect-to-login flow.

As noted above: the regex `%r{/vnc/(\d+)/novnc}` looks for a `/novnc` path segment, but the actual URLs in use are `/vnc/{id}/vnc.html` and `/vnc/{id}/websockify`. This regex will never match those paths, causing `401 Unauthorized` responses for all VNC traffic at the Traefik auth middleware level.

| Property | WeTTY sidecar | noVNC sidecar | Sandbox container |

| Container name | `sc-wetty-{user}-{sandbox}` | `sc-vnc-{user}-{sandbox}` | `{user}-{sandbox}` |
| Image | `wettyoss/wetty:latest` | `gotget/novnc:latest` | `ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle-sandbox:latest` |
| Network | `sandcastle-web` | `sandcastle-web` | default bridge + dynamically joined to `sandcastle-web` on open |
| Port | 3000 (WeTTY HTTP/WS) | 6080 (noVNC HTTP + websockify) | 22 (SSH, host-bound), 5900 (VNC, internal only) |
| Traefik config file | `terminal-{id}.yml` | `vnc-{id}.yml` | none |
| Strip prefix | no | yes (`/vnc/{id}`) | n/a |
| Auth middleware target | `/terminal/auth` | `/vnc/auth` | n/a |
| Auth mechanism | Rails session cookie | Rails session cookie | SSH public key |
| SSH key injection | yes (ed25519, via exec+base64) | no | yes (via env var at startup) |
| Resource limits | 128 MB, 0.5 CPU | 128 MB, 0.25 CPU | none set by manager |
| Restart policy | `no` | `no` | `unless-stopped` |
| Orphan cleanup | yes (by label `sandcastle.sandbox_id`) | yes (by label) | via `ContainerSyncJob` |
| State on disk | `/data/wetty/{full_name}/key[.pub]` | nothing | `/data/users/{name}/home`, `/data/sandboxes/{name}/vol` |

> AGENT

Now for the architecture analysis:

---

## Can the sidecars move into the sandbox (or Rails)?

### What each sidecar actually does

**noVNC sidecar (`sc-vnc-*`):**
1. Serves noVNC static HTML/JS files (web server)
2. Proxies WebSocket → raw TCP RFB to `sandbox:5900` (websockify)

**WeTTY sidecar (`sc-wetty-*`):**
1. Serves xterm.js terminal UI (Node.js web server)
2. Opens an SSH connection to the sandbox and bridges it to the browser WebSocket

### Option A: Move into the Rails container

Not recommended. Rails is a request/response app (Puma threads). Long-lived WebSocket proxies would hold threads indefinitely, starving the app. You'd need a separate process manager, Node.js runtime for WeTTY, Python for websockify — the Rails image becomes a bloated multi-runtime container. This is the wrong direction.

### Option B: Move into the sandbox itself ✅

Both sidecars connect **to** the sandbox. The only reason they're separate containers is that today they need to be on `sandcastle-web` to reach both the sandbox and Traefik. But the sandbox *is already joined to `sandcastle-web`* the moment a terminal/VNC session opens. There's nothing stopping us from running websockify and a terminal server *inside* the sandbox.

---

## Plan

### Part 1: Eliminate the noVNC sidecar

**How it works today:** `sc-vnc-*` container runs `websockify --web /novnc 6080 sandbox:5900`

**New:** The sandbox itself runs `websockify --web /usr/share/novnc 6080 localhost:5900` from entrypoint.sh. Traefik routes directly to `sandbox:6080` instead of `sc-vnc-sandbox:6080`. VncManager drops all container lifecycle code — it just connects the sandbox to `sandcastle-web` and writes the Traefik config.

**Changes:**

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `images/sandbox/Dockerfile` | Add `novnc websockify` to apt-get |
| `images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh` | Start `websockify --web /usr/share/novnc 6080 localhost:5900` after Xvnc |
| `app/services/vnc_manager.rb` | Remove `pull_image`, `create_novnc_container`, `cleanup_orphaned` container logic; Traefik service URL changes from `sc-vnc-{name}:6080` → `{name}:6080` |

**Security:** No change. websockify was already inside the logical sandbox boundary (it can only reach port 5900 on the sandbox). Running it inside the container instead of next to it removes one network hop and one attack surface (the gotget/novnc container itself). Auth is still forwardAuth → Rails.

**UX win:** Session opens in ~1s instead of 3-4s (no container pull/create/start).

---

### Part 2: Eliminate the WeTTY sidecar

**How it works today:** `sc-wetty-*` container SSHs into the sandbox with an ephemeral ed25519 keypair and bridges the connection to the browser via WebSocket. Requires: keypair generation, public key injection via `docker exec`, private key copy via `docker exec`, container start, 2s sleep.

**New:** [`ttyd`](https://github.com/tsl0922/ttyd) — a tiny C binary (~2 MB, no Node.js) that runs a command and exposes it over WebSocket with its own xterm.js UI. Runs inside the sandbox as the sandbox user. Traefik routes directly to `sandbox:7681`. TerminalManager drops all container and SSH key logic.

```bash
# entrypoint.sh — started as the sandbox user after user creation
su -c "ttyd -p 7681 tmux new-session -A -s main" "$USERNAME" &
```

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `images/sandbox/Dockerfile` | Add `ttyd` (apt or binary from GitHub releases) |
| `images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh` | Start ttyd as `$USERNAME` after user creation |
| `app/services/terminal_manager.rb` | Remove keypair generation, key injection, key cleanup, WeTTY container lifecycle; Traefik service URL changes to `{name}:7681`; remove `remove_pubkey` |
| `app/controllers/terminal_controller.rb` | Update auth regex from `/terminal/(\d+)/wetty` → `/terminal/(\d+)` |

**Security analysis:**

| | WeTTY (current) | ttyd (proposed) |
|---|---|---|
| Auth to sandbox | SSH keypair (ephemeral, per-session) | None (direct shell) |
| Auth to browser | Traefik forwardAuth → Rails session | Traefik forwardAuth → Rails session |
| Network exposure | Internal Docker network only | Internal Docker network only |
| Process isolation | Separate container | Inside sandbox |

WeTTY's SSH layer provides defense-in-depth: even if Traefik forwardAuth were bypassed, the attacker would still need the ephemeral private key. With ttyd that layer is gone. However:
- The forwardAuth is cryptographically tied to a Rails session cookie (signed, HttpOnly)  
- The sandbox is the user's own container — there's no cross-user risk
- ttyd is isolated inside the sandbox, not accessible from outside the Docker network

**Verdict: no meaningful security regression.** The SSH hop was protecting against an attacker who had already bypassed Traefik auth — an unlikely and already-dangerous scenario.

**UX win:** Session opens nearly instantly (ttyd already running). No SSH key generation, no `docker exec` chain, no 2s sleep.

---

### Summary

Both are fully feasible with no meaningful security implications. The sidecars exist today only because websockify and WeTTY weren't originally in the sandbox image — not for any architectural reason.

```
Before:  browser → Traefik → sc-wetty/sc-vnc container → sandbox
After:   browser → Traefik → sandbox (directly)
```

Fewer containers, faster session open, less state to clean up (no orphaned sidecar containers), simpler managers. The `ContainerSyncJob` orphan cleanup for WeTTY/VNC containers can be removed entirely.

Want me to implement both phases?

> AGENT

The postgres data is in a **named Docker volume** `pgdata`. The `--volumes` flag on `docker compose down` destroys it. The fix: remove `--volumes` from that call (pgdata is the only named volume; everything else is bind-mounted). Then add a note about it in the preserved-data warning.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_014EwbVvv5QQvQUkdws1T1RK
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/installer/installer.sh.in",
  "old_string": "    if [ -f \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/docker-compose.yml\" ]; then\n      $DOCKER compose -f \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/docker-compose.yml\" --env-file \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/.env\" down --rmi all --volumes --remove-orphans 2>/dev/null || true\n    fi",
  "new_string": "    if [ -f \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/docker-compose.yml\" ]; then\n      # Do NOT pass --volumes: the pgdata named volume holds the postgres database\n      # and must survive uninstall so data is preserved across reinstalls.\n      $DOCKER compose -f \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/docker-compose.yml\" --env-file \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/.env\" down --rmi all --remove-orphans 2>/dev/null || true\n    fi"
}
```

> AGENT

The installer already uses `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/postgres` for secrets and creates the directory. The plan is:

1. Change `docker-compose.yml` to use a bind mount `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/postgres/data:/var/lib/postgresql` instead of the named volume `pgdata`
2. Remove the `pgdata:` named volume declaration
3. Update the installer to create the `data/postgres/data` directory with correct permissions
4. Update the pgdata warning in the installer (it's no longer needed since it's a bind mount)

Done. Here's what changed:

**`docker-compose.yml`** (dev/direct deploy):
- `pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql` → `/data/postgres:/var/lib/postgresql` (bind mount)
- Removed the `volumes: pgdata:` section entirely

**`installer/templates/docker-compose.yml.template`** (was already correct):
- Already used `${SANDCASTLE_HOME}/data/postgres:/var/lib/postgresql`

**`installer/installer.sh.in`** + regenerated **`installer.sh`**:
- `ensure_dirs` now creates `data/postgres` along with the other data dirs
- Updated the `--volumes` comment to explain the bind-mount reason
- Legacy `pgdata` volume check kept but marked as "legacy" for existing installs that had the old named volume

Postgres data now lives at `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/postgres` on disk — fully under operator control and unaffected by any Docker volume management.

> AGENT

Found it. Line 535: `userdel -r "$SANDCASTLE_USER"` — the `-r` flag removes the user's home directory recursively. Since `SANDCASTLE_USER=sandcastle` has home dir set to `$SANDCASTLE_HOME=/sandcastle`, it wipes everything including all data dirs. The explicit `rmdir` calls afterwards are already dead.

> AGENT

Fixed and pushed. The root cause: `userdel -r sandcastle` recursively deletes the sandcastle user's home directory, which is `/sandcastle` — taking all data with it. Changed to plain `userdel` (no `-r`); the explicit `rm`/`rmdir` calls that follow already handle the selective cleanup.

> AGENT

The problem is clear: when dockerd stops (via systemd or directly), only the main `BRIDGE` (`dy_docker0`) is removed. The user-defined Docker network bridges (`br-*`) in the pool CIDR range are left behind in the kernel. On reinstall, the overlap check fires.

The fix: add pool bridge cleanup to both `cmd_stop` and `cmd_destroy`:

> TOOL

Now I have enough context. I'll add a `cleanup_pool_bridges` helper and call it from both `cmd_stop` and `cmd_destroy`:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01GeFPbk2fihycTS8B4WHY97
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/dockyard/dockyard.sh",
  "old_string": "wait_for_file() {",
  "new_string": "cleanup_pool_bridges() {\n    # Remove leftover kernel bridge interfaces (br-*) whose IP falls within\n    # DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE. When dockerd exits, it does not clean up user-defined\n    # network bridges. Left behind, they cause \"overlaps with existing routes\"\n    # errors on the next install because the pool CIDR is still in the routing table.\n    local pool_base=\"${DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE:-}\"\n    [ -n \"$pool_base\" ] || return 0\n\n    # Extract the first two octets of the pool base (e.g. \"10.89\" from \"10.89.0.0/16\")\n    local pool_prefix\n    pool_prefix=$(echo \"$pool_base\" | grep -oP '^\\d+\\.\\d+')\n\n    local removed=0\n    while IFS= read -r iface; do\n        [[ \"$iface\" == br-* ]] || continue\n        local iface_ip\n        iface_ip=$(ip addr show \"$iface\" 2>/dev/null | grep -oP 'inet \\K[^/]+' | head -1)\n        if [[ -n \"$iface_ip\" && \"$iface_ip\" == ${pool_prefix}.* ]]; then\n            echo \"Removing leftover pool bridge: ${iface} (${iface_ip})\"\n            ip link set \"$iface\" down 2>/dev/null || true\n            ip link delete \"$iface\" 2>/dev/null || true\n            removed=$((removed + 1))\n        fi\n    done < <(ip link show type bridge 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '^\\d+: \\K[^:@]+')\n\n    [ \"$removed\" -gt 0 ] || true\n}\n\nwait_for_file() {"
}
```

diff --git a/dockyard.sh b/dockyard.sh
index 8e78b68..8c871e5 100755
--- a/dockyard.sh
+++ b/dockyard.sh
@@ -102,6 +102,34 @@ stop_daemon() {
     echo "  ${name} stopped"
 }
 
+cleanup_pool_bridges() {
+    # Remove leftover kernel bridge interfaces (br-*) whose IP falls within
+    # DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE. When dockerd exits, it does not clean up user-defined
+    # network bridges. Left behind, they cause "overlaps with existing routes"
+    # errors on the next install because the pool CIDR is still in the routing table.
+    local pool_base="${DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE:-}"
+    [ -n "$pool_base" ] || return 0
+
+    # Extract the first two octets of the pool base (e.g. "10.89" from "10.89.0.0/16")
+    local pool_prefix
+    pool_prefix=$(echo "$pool_base" | grep -oP '^\d+\.\d+')
+
+    local removed=0
+    while IFS= read -r iface; do
+        [[ "$iface" == br-* ]] || continue
+        local iface_ip
+        iface_ip=$(ip addr show "$iface" 2>/dev/null | grep -oP 'inet \K[^/]+' | head -1)
+        if [[ -n "$iface_ip" && "$iface_ip" == ${pool_prefix}.* ]]; then
+            echo "Removing leftover pool bridge: ${iface} (${iface_ip})"
+            ip link set "$iface" down 2>/dev/null || true
+            ip link delete "$iface" 2>/dev/null || true
+            removed=$((removed + 1))
+        fi
+    done < <(ip link show type bridge 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '^\d+: \K[^:@]+')
+
+    [ "$removed" -gt 0 ] || true
+}
+
 wait_for_file() {
     local file="$1"
     local label="$2"
@@ -623,6 +651,9 @@ cmd_stop() {
         echo "Bridge ${BRIDGE} removed"
     fi
 
+    # Remove leftover user-defined network bridges from the pool range
+    cleanup_pool_bridges
+
     echo "=== All daemons stopped ==="
 }
 
@@ -957,6 +988,9 @@ cmd_destroy() {
         sleep 2
     fi
 
+    # --- 1.5. Remove leftover user-defined network bridges from the pool ---

> AGENT

Done. Root cause and fix:

**Root cause:** When dockerd exits, it leaves behind the kernel `br-*` bridge interfaces it created for user-defined Docker networks (like `sandcastle-web`, Tailscale bridges, etc.). Only the main dockyard bridge (`dy_docker0`) was removed on stop/destroy. The orphaned `br-*` interfaces kept their IPs in the routing table, so on reinstall the `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` overlap check failed.

**Fix:** Added `cleanup_pool_bridges()` to `dockyard.sh` — after stopping dockerd, it scans all kernel bridge interfaces and removes any `br-*` whose IP falls within the `DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE` prefix. Called from both `cmd_stop` and `cmd_destroy`.

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

The remount fails even manually as root in a sysbox container. Let me check what kind of mount `/dev/shm` actually is inside the container — that'll tell us why `remount` fails:

Can you run inside the container:
```bash
cat /proc/mounts | grep shm
```

But I already know enough to fix it. `remount,size=` only works if the existing mount is a real tmpfs owned by this namespace. Sysbox likely bind-mounts `/dev/shm` from the runtime, making `remount` invalid. The fix is to unmount it and mount a fresh tmpfs:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_011fU9E8TLhxwz6a99gLbhUj
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh",
  "old_string": "# Resize /dev/shm to 2GB for Chrome — Sysbox gives each container an isolated\n# kernel so remounting tmpfs works. Docker's ShmSize HostConfig key is not\n# supported by sysbox-runc, so we do it here instead.\nmount -o remount,size=2g /dev/shm 2>/dev/null || true",
  "new_string": "# Resize /dev/shm to 2GB for Chrome. Docker's ShmSize HostConfig key is not\n# supported by sysbox-runc, so we do it here instead. The runtime bind-mounts\n# /dev/shm from a small constrained shm, so \"remount,size=\" fails; unmounting\n# it and mounting a fresh tmpfs works.\numount /dev/shm 2>/dev/null || true\nmount -t tmpfs -o size=2g,mode=1777 tmpfs /dev/shm 2>/dev/null || true"
}
```

```json
{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(sandbox): use umount+mount instead of remount for /dev/shm\n\nremount,size= fails inside Sysbox because the runtime bind-mounts /dev/shm\nfrom a constrained shm rather than an independent tmpfs — the kernel rejects\nthe size change with EINVAL. Unmounting and mounting a fresh 2 GB tmpfs\nworks around this.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" -- images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh && git push",
  "description": "Commit and push shm fix"
}
```

  "content": "#!/bin/bash\n# Sandcastle wrapper for Google Chrome — sets up the display and required flags\n# so Chrome works inside a Docker/Sysbox container with a VNC virtual display.\necho \"[sandcastle] google-chrome wrapper: setting DISPLAY=:99 and required Docker flags\" >&2\n\nmkdir -p /tmp/chrome-config /tmp/chrome-cache\n\nexport REDACTED\nexport XDG_CACHE_HOME=/tmp/chrome-cache\n\n# Call the real binary directly to avoid recursion (this script shadows\n# /usr/bin/google-chrome via /usr/local/bin which is earlier in PATH).\nexec /opt/google/chrome/google-chrome \\\n  --display=:99 \\\n  --no-sandbox \\\n  --disable-dev-shm-usage \\\n  --disable-gpu \\\n  --disable-software-rasterizer \\\n  \"$@\"\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. Note though: Hyprland is a **Wayland** compositor and the sandbox VNC runs on **X11** (Xvnc :99). Hyprland won't launch under X11 — it needs a Wayland session. You'd need something like a nested Wayland compositor (e.g. `weston` or `cage`) running inside the X11 display, or switch the VNC setup to use a Wayland-native VNC server.

Also start openbox in the entrypoint after Xvnc is up:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_019Gd42ZUeM1MFT6hxaZHhFZ
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/images/sandbox/entrypoint.sh",
  "old_string": "if command -v Xvnc &>/dev/null; then\n    Xvnc :99 -rfbport 5900 -SecurityTypes None -AlwaysShared \\\n        -geometry 1920x1080 -depth 24 \\\n        &>/var/log/xvnc.log &\nfi",
  "new_string": "if command -v Xvnc &>/dev/null; then\n    Xvnc :99 -rfbport 5900 -SecurityTypes None -AlwaysShared \\\n        -geometry 1920x1080 -depth 24 \\\n        &>/var/log/xvnc.log &\n    # Start Openbox window manager once the display is ready\n    if command -v openbox &>/dev/null; then\n        DISPLAY=:99 openbox &>/var/log/openbox.log &\n    fi\nfi"
}
```
